u^{n-1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}&u^{n}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\end{array}\right)

Proof.

The proof is by induction on
n
. The result is valid for
n=1
. Suppose true for
n
and let us prove for
n+1
. We have that

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}us&u^{2}t\\
1&0\end{array}\right)^{n+1}

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}us&u^{2}t\\
1&0\end{array}\right)^{n}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}us&u^{2}t\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u^{n}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}&u^{n+1}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\\
u^{n-1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}&u^{n}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\end{array}\right)\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}us&u^{2}t\\
1&0\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u^{n+1}(s\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}+t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t})&u^{n+2}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\\
u^{n}(s\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}+t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t})&u^{n+1}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\end{array}\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}u^{n+1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}&u^{n+2}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\\
u^{n}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}&u^{n+1}t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\end{array}\right)

and the statement turns out to be true for all
n
.
∎

From Theorems
1
and
2
we have the following results.

Theorem 3
.

For all
u\geq 0
,

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}us&u^{2}t\\
1&0\end{array}\right)^{n}=\begin{cases}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}0&0\\
0&0\end{array}\right),&\text{ if }0\leq u<\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}\text{ or }0\leq u<\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1};\\
\frac{1}{\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\varphi_{s,t}&t\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}\\
\varphi_{s,t}&t\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}\end{array}\right),&\text{ if }u=\varphi_{s,t}^{-1};\\
\frac{-1}{\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}&t\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}\\
\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}&t\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}\end{array}\right),&\text{ if }u=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}.\end{cases}

From all of the above we can conclude that

\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\overset{\rightarrow}{F}_{n}=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}A^{n}\overset{\rightarrow}{F}_{0}=\begin{cases}(0,0)^{\mathrm{T}},&\text{ if }0\leq u<\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}\text{ or if }0\leq u<\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1};\\
\left(\frac{1}{1-q},\frac{1}{1-q}\right)^{\mathrm{T}},&\text{ if }u=\varphi_{s,t}^{-1};\\
\left(\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}},\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}}\right)^{\mathrm{T}},&\text{ if }u=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1};\\
(\infty,\infty)^{\mathrm{T}},&\text{ if }u>\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}\text{ or if }u>\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}.\end{cases}

Then
(0,0)
,
\left(\frac{1}{1-q},\frac{1}{1-q}\right)
and
\left(\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}},\frac{1}{1-q^{-1}}\right)
are asymptotically stable fixed points of Eq.(
23
), provided that either
u\in[0,\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}]
or
u\in[0,\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}]
. If either
u>\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}
or
u>\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}
, the the solution
\overset{\rightarrow}{F}_{n}
remains unbounded, i.e.,
|\overset{\rightarrow}{F}_{n}|\rightarrow\infty
as
n\rightarrow\infty
. Then
u=\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}
and
u=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime-1}
are bifurcation points of Eq.(
23
). At these points,
u^{n-1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{q}
and
u^{n-1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{q^{-1}}
, respectively, and thus the
q
-numbers become ”bifurcation points” of the family of deformed
(s,t)
-numbers.

3
Calculus on deformed generalized Fibonacci polynomials

3.1
The Ward ring of Fibonomial exponential generating functions

Now let
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[x]]
denote the set of
(s,t)
-exponential generating functions of the form
\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(x^{n}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!)
with coefficients in
\mathbb{C}
. It is clear that
(\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[x]],+,\cdot)
is a ring with sum and product ordinary of series, that is,

f(x)+g(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(a_{n}+b_{n})\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

and

f(x)\cdot g(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\sum_{k=0}^{n}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{k}_{s,t}a_{k}b_{n-k}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!},

where
f(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}(x^{n}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!)
,

g(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}b_{n}(x^{n}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!)\in\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[x]]
.

Definition 2
.

The ring
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
will be called generalized Ward-Fibonomial ring of
(s,t)
-exponential generating functions, or
(s,t)
-Ward ring,

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a_{n}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

with coefficient in
\mathbb{C}
. Let
f_{s,t}(z,u)
denote the function

f_{s,t}(z,u)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a_{n}z^{n}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!

in
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
We will call to
f_{s,t}(z,u)
a deformed function.

Theorem 4
.

Set
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
s\neq 0
,
s^{2}+4t>0
and
(s,t)\neq(2,-1)
. Take
f_{s,t}(z,u)
in
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
, such that
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!\nmid a_{n}
and
u^{-\binom{n}{2}}\nmid a_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
. Set
\alpha=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}|a_{n+1}/a_{n}|\geq 0
.
If
|q|<1
, the function
f_{s,t}(z,u)
is:

1.

An entire function if
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}|
.

2.

Convergent in the disk
|z|<1/\alpha|1-q|
when
\alpha>0
and an entire function when
\alpha=0
, provided that
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}|
.

3.

Convergent in
z=0
when
|u|>|\varphi_{s,t}|
.

Suppose that
|q|>1
. The function
f_{s,t}(z,u)
is:

4.

An entire when
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
.

5.

Convergent in the disk
|z|<1/\alpha|q^{-1}-1|
when
\alpha>0
and an entire function when
\alpha=0
, provided that
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
.

6.

Convergent in
z=0
when
|u|>|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
.

Proof.

We will use the D’alembert test. If
|q|<1
, then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\frac{xu^{n}a_{n+1}}{a_{n}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\alpha|z|\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg|\frac{u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\bigg|

\displaystyle=\alpha|z|\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\frac{(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n+1}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(n+1)}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\alpha|z||1-q|\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg|\frac{1}{1-q^{n+1}}\bigg|\bigg|\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}\bigg|.

Now, follow the first 3 statements. Now suppose that
|q|>1
. Then

\displaystyle\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\Bigg|\frac{xu^{n}a_{n+1}}{a_{n}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=\alpha|z||q^{-1}-1|\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\bigg|\frac{1}{q^{-(n+1)}-1}\bigg|\bigg|\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime n}}\bigg|.

Therefore follow the last three statements.
∎

The above theorem is consistent with Theorem
1
, in the sense that the convergence of a deformed function
f_{s,t}(z,u)
depends strongly on the values taken by the parameter
u
. In the following theorem, we analyze the case
(s,t)=(2,-1)
.

Theorem 5
.

Let
f_{2,-1}(z,u)
denote the function
f_{2,-1}(z,u)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a_{n}z^{n}/n!
with
a_{n}\in\mathbb{C}
such that
n!\nmid a_{n}
and
u^{-\binom{n}{2}}\nmid a_{n}
for all
n\geq 0
. Set
\alpha=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}|a_{n+1}/a_{n}|
. Then
f_{2,-1}(z,u)
is an entire function when
0<u\leq 1
and convergent in
z=0
when
u>1
.

Example 1
.

The function
\frac{1}{1-z}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}z^{n}
is convergent when
|z|<1
. Then by the above theorem,
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits(z,u)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{n!}
is an entire function when
0<u\leq 1
.

Because of what we have seen in the previous section and because of Theorems
4
and
5
, in the remainder of the paper we will restrict ourselves only to deformed functions with deformation parameter
u\in(0,|\varphi_{s,t}|]
and
u\in(0,|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|]
.

3.2
The deformed
(s,t)
-derivative

In this section, we introduce the deformed differential operator. In addition, we introduce the set of
q
-periodic functions and it will be shown that these functions are invariant by deformations.

Definition 3
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
s^{2}+4t>0
. For all
u\in(0,|\varphi_{s,t}|]
or
u\in(0,|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|]
define the deformed
(s,t)
-derivative
\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}
of the function
f(x)
as

(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}f)(x)=\frac{f(u\varphi_{s,t}x)-f(u\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)}{u(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})x}

for all
x\neq 0
and
(\mathbf{D}_{us,u^{2}t}f)(0)=f^{\prime}(0)
, provided
f^{\prime}(0)
exists.

The deformed
(s,t)
-derivative is a particular case of the
(p,q)
-derivative with
p=u\varphi_{s,t}
and
q=u\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}
. Next, we will give the definitions of deformed derivatives according to each specialization.

Definition 4
.

Take
s=1,\ t=1
. For all
u\in(0,\frac{1+\sqrt{5}}{2}]
or
u\in(0,|\frac{1-\sqrt{5}}{2}|]
, we define the deformed Fibonacci derivative

\mathbf{D}_{u,u^{2}}f(x)=\mathbf{D}_{F,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(u\varphi x\right)-f\left(u\varphi^{\prime}x\right)}{u\sqrt{5}x},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 5
.

Take
s=2,\ t=1
. For all
u\in(0,1+\sqrt{2}]
or
u\in(0,|1-\sqrt{2}|]
we define the deformed Pell derivative

\mathbf{D}_{2u,u^{2}}f(x)=\mathbf{D}_{P,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(u(1+\sqrt{2})x\right)-f\left(u(1-\sqrt{2})x\right)}{2u\sqrt{2}x},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 6
.

Take
s=1,\ t=2
. For all
u\in(0,2]
or
u\in(0,1]
we define the deformed Jacobsthal derivative

\mathbf{D}_{u,2u^{2}}f(x)=\mathbf{D}_{J,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(2ux\right)-f\left(-ux\right)}{3ux},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 7
.

Take
s=3,\ t=-2
. For all
u\in(0,2]
or
u\in(0,1]
we define the deformed Mersenne derivative

\mathbf{D}_{u,2u^{2}}f(x)=\mathbf{D}_{M,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(2ux\right)-f\left(ux\right)}{ux},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 8
.

Take
s=p+q,\ t=-pq
. For all
u\in(0,|p|]
or
u\in(0,|q|]
we define the deformed
(p,q)
-derivative

\mathbf{D}_{u(p+q),-pqu^{2}}f(x)=D_{p,q,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(upx\right)-f\left(uqx\right)}{u(p-q)x},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 9
.

Take
s=2r,\lvert r\rvert>1,\ t=-1
. For all
u\in(0,r+\sqrt{r^{2}-1}]
or
u\in(0,|r-\sqrt{r^{2}-1}]
we define the deformed Chebysheff of second kind derivative

\mathbf{D}_{2ru,-u^{2}}f(x)=D_{U,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(u(r+\sqrt{r^{2}-1})x\right)-f\left(u(r-\sqrt{r^{2}-1})x\right)}{2u\sqrt{r^{2}-1}x},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Definition 10
.

Take
s=P,\ t=-Q
. For all

u\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}|P+\sqrt{P^{2}-4Q}|\right]\text{ or }u\in\left(0,\frac{1}{2}|P-\sqrt{P^{2}-4Q}|\right]

we define the deformed Lucas-derivative

\mathbf{D}_{uP,-Qu^{2}}f(x)=D_{P,-Q,u}f(x)=\begin{cases}\frac{f\left(u\varphi_{P,-Q}x\right)-f\left(u\varphi_{P,-Q}^{\prime}x\right)}{u(\varphi_{P,-Q}-\varphi_{P,-Q}^{\prime})x},&\text{ if }x\neq 0;\\
f^{\prime}(0),&\text{ if }x=0.\end{cases}

Example 2
.

For function
f(x)=x^{5}
, deformed
(s,t)
-derivative is obtained as

\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}x^{5}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{5}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{su,tu^{2}}x^{4}=u^{4}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{5}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}x^{4}.

Then,
\mathbf{D}_{F,u}x^{5}=5u^{4}x^{4}
,
\mathbf{D}_{P,u}x^{5}=29u^{4}x^{4}
,
\mathbf{D}_{J,u}x^{5}=5u^{4}x^{4}
,
\mathbf{D}_{M,u}x^{5}=31u^{4}x^{4}
and

\displaystyle D_{U,u}x^{5}

\displaystyle=u^{4}(16t^{4}-12t^{2}+1)x^{4},

\displaystyle D_{P,-Q,u}x^{5}

\displaystyle=u^{4}(P^{4}-3P^{2}Q+Q^{2})x^{4}.

Example 3
.

The ordinary and exponential generating functions of the sequence
(1,1,\ldots)
are transformed into the ordinary and exponential generating functions of the polynomials
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}
, respectively, through the operator in differences
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}

\displaystyle x\mathbf{D}_{s,t}\left(\frac{1}{1-x}\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{x}{1-sx-tx^{2}},

\displaystyle x\mathbf{D}_{s,t}e^{x}

\displaystyle=\frac{e^{\varphi_{s,t}x}-e^{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x}}{\sqrt{s^{2}+4t}}.

For two fixed complex numbers
s,t
the ring
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
is equipped with the family of derivatives
\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}
, for every nonzero complex number
u
. In particular, in the ring
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
, we define the following derivatives

D_{q}=\mathbf{D}_{1+q,-q}=\mathbf{D}_{s(1/\varphi_{s,t}),t(1/\varphi_{s,t})^{2}}

(24)

and

D_{U}=\mathbf{D}_{2(is/2\sqrt{t}),-1}=\mathbf{D}_{s(i/\sqrt{t}),t(i/\sqrt{t})^{2}}.

(25)

The following results on the deformed
(s,t)
-derivative are standard:

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}(f(x)+g(x))

\displaystyle=(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}f)(x)+(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}g)(x),

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}(\alpha f(x))

\displaystyle=\alpha(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}f)(x),\text{ for all }\alpha\in\mathbb{C},

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}(f(x)g(x))

\displaystyle=f(u\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}g)(x)+g(u\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}f)(x),

(26)

\displaystyle=f(u\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}g)(x)+g(u\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{su,tu^{2}}f)(x).

(27)

Definition 11
.

We will say that the function
f(x)
is
q
-periodic if
f(y)=f(qy)
, with
y=\varphi_{s,t}x
. Let
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
denote the set of
q
-periodic functions. The
q
-periodic functions form the kernel of the operator
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
t<0
. From the condition of
q
-periodicity of
f(x)
it follows that
f(y)=f(qy)
, with
q>0
. Then

\displaystyle f(q^{y})

\displaystyle=f(q^{y+1})

\displaystyle G(y)

\displaystyle=G(y+1)

where
G
is an arbitrary periodic function with period one and
f(x)=G(\log_{q}(x))
,
x>0
and
\log_{q}(x)
is the logarithm function in base
q
. Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))-G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x))}{(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})x}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))-G(\log_{q}(q\varphi_{s,t}x))}{(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})x}

\displaystyle=\frac{G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))-G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))}{(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})x}=0

and
f(x)\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
. If
s\neq 0
and
t>0
, then
q<0
and
f(x)=G(\log(x)/(\log(-q)+i\pi))
, so that
x\in\mathbb{C}/\{0\}
and
f(x)\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
.
As
q=\frac{\varphi_{su,tu^{2}}^{\prime}}{\varphi_{su,tu^{2}}}=\frac{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}{\varphi_{s,t}}
, every function in
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
is invariant by deformations.
Thus
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}=\mathbb{P}_{su,tu^{2}}
. Now, set
s\neq 0
and
t=0
. Then
\mathbb{P}_{s,0}=\mathbb{C}
.

In the following theorem, we will show that the set of
\varphi_{s,t}
-periodic functions is an algebra.

Theorem 6
.

The set
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
is a
\mathbb{C}
-algebra.

Proof.

If
f(x),g(x)\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
, then

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(f(x)+g(x))

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(f(x)g(x))

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(\alpha f(x))

\displaystyle=0

for all
\alpha\in\mathbb{C}
. Then
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
is closed concerning sum, product of functions, and product by a scalar. Now the properties of
\mathbb{C}
-algebra follow easily.
∎

If
f(x)\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
, then

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(f(x)g(x))=f(\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x)=f(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x).

In
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[x]]
the only function satisfying
(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)=0
is the constant function
f(x)=C
. Then
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}\cap\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[x]]=\mathbb{C}
.

3.3
The
(s,t)
-antiderivative

Let
f
be an arbitrary function. In
[
35
]
the following
(p,q)
-integrals of
f
in the interval
[0,a]
are defined

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{a}f(x)d_{p,q}x

\displaystyle=(p-q)a\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{q^{k}}{p^{k+1}}f\left(\frac{q^{k}}{p^{k+1}}a\right)\text{ if }\ \Bigg|\frac{p}{q}\Bigg|>1,

(28)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{a}f(x)d_{p,q}x

\displaystyle=(q-p)a\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{p^{k}}{q^{k+1}}f\left(\frac{p^{k}}{q^{k+1}}a\right)\text{ if }\ \Bigg|\frac{p}{q}\Bigg|<1

(29)

and the
(p,q)
-integral of
f
in the interval
[a,b]
is defined as

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{p,q}x

\displaystyle=\int_{0}^{b}f(x)d_{p,q}x-\int_{0}^{a}f(x)d_{p,q}x.

Let
I
denote a set of real numbers. Set
q=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}/\varphi_{s,t}
such that
0<|q|<1
. We will say that
I
is a
(q,\varphi_{s,t})
-geometric set if it contains all geometric sequences
\{xq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t}\}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}=\mathbb{N}\cup\{0\}
,
x\in I
. For a pair of real numbers
a
and
b
, we define

[a,b]_{\varphi}=\Bigg\{a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Bigg\}\cup\Bigg\{b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Bigg\}

which we will call a
(q,\varphi_{s,t})
-interval with extreme points
a
and
b
. For real numbers
a
and
b
, the following property holds:

\text{If }a,b\in I\ \Longrightarrow\ [a,b]_{\varphi}\subset I.

Consider the function
f:[a,b]_{\varphi}\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
, where
\mathbb{K}
is either
\mathbb{R}
or
\mathbb{C}
. The
(s,t)
-integral in
[a,b]_{\varphi}
of
f
is defined as

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[bf\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)-af\left(a\frac{Q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]q^{n}.

(30)

Equally, set
q
such that
|q|>1
. We will say that
I
is a
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})
-geometric set if it contains all geometric sequences
\{xq^{-n}/\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}\}
,
n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
,
x\in I
. Similarly, we define the
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})
-interval with extreme points
a
and
b

[a,b]_{\varphi^{\prime}}=\Bigg\{a\frac{q^{-n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Bigg\}\cup\Bigg\{b\frac{q^{-n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Bigg\}.

The
(s,t)
-integral in
[a,b]_{\varphi^{\prime}}
of
f
is

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[bf\left(b\frac{q^{-n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\right)-af\left(a\frac{q^{-n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}}\right)\Bigg]q^{-n}.

(31)

If
[a,b]_{q}
and
[a,b]_{q^{-1}}
denote respectively the
q
-interval and
q^{-1}
-interval,

\displaystyle[a,b]_{q}

\displaystyle=\Big\{aq^{n}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Big\}\cup\Big\{bq^{n}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Big\},

\displaystyle[a,b]_{q^{-1}}

\displaystyle=\Big\{aq^{-n}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Big\}\cup\Big\{bq^{-n}\ :\ n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}\Big\},

then
[a,b]_{\varphi}=[a/\varphi_{s,t},b/\varphi_{s,t}]_{q}
shows the relationship between
(q,\varphi_{s,t})
-intervals and
q
-intervals. Likewise,
[a,b]_{\varphi^{\prime}}=[a/\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime},b/\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}]_{q^{-1}}
.
From
[
35
]
it is known that if
0<q<1
and if
|f(x)x^{\alpha}|
is bounded on the interval
(0,A]
for some
0\leq\alpha<1
, then the
(p,q)
-integrals Eqs.(
28
) and (
29
) convergent to a function
F(x)
on
(0,A]
, which is a
(p,q)
-antiderivative of
f(x)
, i.e.,

D_{p,q}\int_{0}^{x}f(r)d_{p,q}r=f(x).

In the following result, it is clear that a
(s,t)
-integral under deformation is equal to re-scaling the integration interval.

Proposition 3
.

Set
u\neq 0
. For
q\neq 1
,

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{us,u^{2}t}x=u\int_{a/u}^{b/u}f(x)d_{s,t}x.

Proof.

By Definition
30
,

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{us,u^{2}t}x

\displaystyle=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[bf\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{u\varphi_{s,t}}\right)-af\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{u\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]q^{n}

\displaystyle=u(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[\frac{b}{u}f\left(\frac{b}{u}\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)-\frac{a}{u}f\left(\frac{a}{u}\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]q^{n}

\displaystyle=u\int_{a/u}^{b/u}f(x)d_{s,t}x.

The same result follows by applying Eq.(
31
).
∎

Corollary 1
.

1.

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x=\varphi_{s,t}\int_{a/\varphi_{s,t}}^{b/\varphi_{s,t}}f(x)d_{q}x

2.

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x=\frac{i}{\sqrt{t}}\int_{-a\sqrt{t}i}^{-b\sqrt{t}i}f(x)d_{U}x

The
(s,t)
-analog of the property of the ordinary integral

\int_{a}^{b}\alpha f(x)dx=\alpha\int_{a}^{b}f(x)dx,\ \alpha\in\mathbb{R}

is given below.

Theorem 7
.

Set
q\neq 1
. For all function
G(\log_{q}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
and for all function
f

(s,t)
-integrable in
[0,r]
,
r\in\{a,b\}
,
0\leq a<b
, and continuous at
0
, it is true that

\int_{a}^{b}G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)d_{s,t}x=\Bigg[G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{r}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\int_{0}^{r}f(x)d_{s,t}x\Bigg]_{r=a}^{b}.

Proof.

Suppose
0<a<b
. By Definition
30
with
0<|q|<1

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)d_{s,t}x

\displaystyle=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[bG\left(\log_{q}\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt-aG\left(\log_{q}\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]q^{n}.

Now, for
q
-periodicity
G(\log_{q}(rq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t}))=G(\log_{q}(r/\varphi_{s,t}))
, for
r\in\{a,b\}
. Then

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)d_{s,t}x

\displaystyle=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[bG\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{b}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-aG\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{a}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]q^{n}

\displaystyle=(1-q)G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{b}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}bf\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)q^{n}

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-(1-q)G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{a}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}af\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)q^{n}

\displaystyle=G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{b}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\int_{0}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt-G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{a}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\int_{0}^{a}f(x)d_{s,t}x.

If
a=0
, then

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{b}G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)d_{s,t}x

\displaystyle=(1-q)b\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}G\left(\log_{q}\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)q^{n}

\displaystyle=(1-q)bG\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{b}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}f\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)q^{n}

\displaystyle=G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{b}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)\int_{0}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x.

∎

The following result is of fundamental importance for the theory that will be developed below. For
q
-integral we can see
[
52
]
.

Proposition 4
.

Let
f
and
g
be two
(s,t)
-integrable functions in
[a,b]
such that
f(x)\leq g(x)
for all
x\in[a,b]_{\varphi}
. If
a\leq 0\leq b
, then

\int_{a}^{b}f(x)d_{s,t}x\leq\int_{a}^{b}g(x)d_{s,t}x.

(32)

Proof.

By hypothesis the inequalities

f(bq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})\leq g(bq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})

and

f(aq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})\leq g(aq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})

hold for all
n=0,1,2,.\ldots
. Therefore, since
a\leq 0\leq b
, we obtain

bf(bq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})-af(aq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})\leq bg(bq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t})-ag(aq^{n}/\varphi_{s,t}),

n=0,1,2,\ldots
. Thus Eq.(
32
) follows from Eq.(
30
).
∎

Corollary 2
.

If
a\leq 0\leq b
and
|f|
is
(s,t)
-integrable in
[a,b]
, then
f
is
(s,t)
-integrable in
[a,b]
and the following inequality holds:

\Bigg|\int_{a}^{b}fd_{s,t}\Bigg|\leq\int_{a}^{b}|f|d_{s,t}.

Proof.

Follows from Proposition
4
by taking into account that the inequalities
-|f(x)|\leq f(x)\leq|f(x)|
hold for all
x\in[a,b]_{\varphi}
.
∎

Definition 12
.

For any real number
p\geq 1
and
a,b\in I
, we will denote by
\mathcal{L}_{s,t}^{p}[a,b]
the set of functions
f:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
such that
|f|^{p}
is
(s,t)
-integrable in
[a,b]
, i.e.,

\mathcal{L}_{s,t}^{p}[a,b]=\Bigg\{f:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}\ :\ \int_{a}^{b}|f|^{p}d_{s,t}<\infty\Bigg\}.

We also set

\mathcal{L}_{s,t}^{\infty}[a,b]=\Bigg\{f:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}\ :\ \sup_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}\Bigg\{\Bigg|f\left(\frac{aq^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg|,\Bigg|f\left(\frac{bq^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg|\Bigg\}<\infty\Bigg\}.

Theorem 8

(
The fundamental theorem of
(s,t)
-calculus).

Let
0<|q|<1
, and
I=[a,b]
a
(q,\varphi)
-interval. Fix
a,b\in I
, and let
f:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
be a function such that
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f\in\mathcal{L}_{s,t}^{1}[a,b]
. Then:

1.

The equality

\int_{a}^{b}(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)d_{s,t}x=\Bigg[f(r)-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}f\left(rq^{n}\right)\Bigg]_{r=a}^{b}

holds, provided the involved limits exist.

2.

In addition, if
f
is continuous at
0
, then

\int_{a}^{b}(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)d_{s,t}x=f(b)-f(a).

Proof.

Since the equality

r(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)\left(r\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)=\frac{f\left(rq^{n}\right)-f(rq^{n+1})}{(1-q)q^{n}}

holds for
r\in\{a,b\}
, we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{a}^{b}(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)d_{s,t}x

\displaystyle=(1-q)\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\Bigg[b(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)\left(b\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)-a(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)\left(a\frac{q^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\Bigg]

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left((f(bq^{n})-f(aq^{n}))-(f(bq^{n+1})-f(aq^{n+1}))\right)

\displaystyle=\Bigg[f(r)-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}f\left(rq^{n}\right)\Bigg]_{r=a}^{b}

where we have used the telescoping property and the hypothesis that the limits
\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}f(rq^{n})
exist for
r\in\{a,b\}
.
∎

Theorem 9

(
The
(s,t)
-integration by parts formula).

Assume
0<|q|<1
, and let
I=[a,b]
be a
(q,\varphi)
-interval. Let
f:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
and
g:I\rightarrow\mathbb{K}
. For
a,b\in I
, the equality

\int_{I}(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)g(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)d_{s,t}x=\Big[(f\cdot g)(s)-\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}(f\cdot g)(sq^{n})\Big]_{s=a}^{b}\\
-\int_{I}f(\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x)d_{s,t}x

(33)

holds, provided
f,g\in\mathcal{L}_{s,t}^{1}[a,b]
,
(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)
and
(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x)
are bounded for all
x\in[a,b]_{\varphi}
, and the limits exists. If, in addition,
f
and
g
are continuous at
0
, then

\int_{I}(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)g(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)d_{s,t}x=\Big[f\cdot g\Big]_{a}^{b}-\int_{I}f(\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x)d_{s,t}x.

Proof.

By the
(s,t)
-product rules, Eqs.(
26
) and (
27
) one has

g(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f)(x)=\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(f(x)g(x))-f(\varphi_{s,t}x)(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}g)(x),

hence, integrating both sides of this equality over the interval
[a,b]
and taking into account Theorem
8
, the result follows.
∎

Analogous results to the previous ones are obtained when
|q|>1
.

3.4
Deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function

In this section, the definition of deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function along with its analytical properties are given.

Definition 13
.

Set
s\neq 0
. For all
u\in\mathbb{C}
, we define the deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function in
\mathop{\mbox{{W}}}\nolimits_{s,t,\mathbb{C}}[[z]]
as

\exp_{s,t}(z,u)=\begin{cases}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}&\text{ if }u\neq 0;\\
1+z&\text{ if }u=0.\end{cases}

Also, we define

\displaystyle\exp_{s,t}(z)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(z,1),

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(z,\varphi_{s,t}),

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits^{\prime}_{s,t}(z)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(z,\varphi^{\prime}_{s,t}).

Definition 14
.

Set
s,t\in\mathbb{R}/\{0\}
. We define the deformed
q
-exponential and the deformed
U
-exponential functions, respectively, as

\displaystyle\exp_{q}(z,u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left[\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left]\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{q}!},

\displaystyle\exp_{2\tau,-1}(z,u)

\displaystyle=1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{\sin^{n}(\theta_{s,t})}{\sin(\theta_{s,t})\sin(2\theta_{s,t})\cdots\sin(n\theta_{s,t})}z^{n}

with
\tau=is/2\sqrt{t}
and
\theta_{s,t}=\arccos(\tau)\neq k\pi
,
k\in\mathbb{Z}
. When
s\neq 0
and
t=0
,

\exp_{s,0}(z,u)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{u}{s}\right)^{\binom{n}{2}}z^{n}.

If
u=s

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,0}(z)=\frac{1}{1-z}\text{ and }\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,0}^{\prime}(z)=1+z.

From the above definition, it is very easy to establish that

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)

\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(z),

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)

\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(z,q),

\displaystyle\exp_{s,t}(z,u)

\displaystyle=\exp_{q}(z,u/\varphi_{s,t}).

where
q=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}/\varphi_{s,t}
.

The following Theorem follows from Theorem
4
.

Theorem 10
.

Set
s\neq 0,t\neq 0
. If
|q|<1
, the function
\exp_{s,t}(z,u)
is

1.

an entire function if
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}|
,

2.

convergent in the disk
|z|<|\varphi_{s,t}|/\sqrt{s^{2}+4t}
when
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}|
,

3.

convergent in
z=0
when
|u|>|\varphi_{s,t}|
.

Suppose that
|q|>1
. The function
\exp_{s,t}(z,u)
is

4.

is entire when
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
,

5.

convergent in the disk
|z|<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|/\sqrt{s^{2}+4t}
when
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
,

6.

convergent in
z=0
when
|u|>|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
.

It is very straightforward to obtain the following result.

Theorem 11
.

For
s\neq 0,t=0
the function
\exp_{s,0}(az,u)
,
a\neq 0
, is

1.

an entire function if
|u|<|s|
,

2.

convergent in the disk
|z|<1/|a|
when
|u|=|s|
,

3.

convergent in
z=0
when
|u|>|s|
.

Proposition 5
.

For all
u,v\in\mathbb{C}
,
v\neq 0
, and
t\neq 0
the deformed
(s,t)
-exponential functions holds

\exp_{vs,v^{2}t}(z,u)=\exp_{s,t}(z,u/v).

If
v=u
, then
\exp_{us,u^{2}t}(z,u)=\exp_{s,t}(z)
.

Proof.

The proof is as follows

\displaystyle\exp_{vs,v^{2}t}(z,u)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{vs,v^{2}t}!}=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{v^{\binom{n}{2}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}=\exp_{s,t}(z,u/v).

If we make
v=u
, then
\exp_{us,u^{2}t}(z,u)=\exp_{s,t}(z)
.
∎

Theorem 12
.

The deformed derivative of the deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function is

\mathbf{D}_{sv,tv^{2}}(\exp_{s,t}(z,u))=\exp_{s,t}(uvz,u).

Proof.

We have that

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{sv,tv^{2}}(\exp_{s,t}(z,u))

\displaystyle=\mathbf{D}_{sv,tv^{2}}\left(\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n}}{v^{-\binom{n}{2}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}!}\right)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{\mathbf{D}_{sv,tv^{2}}\left(z^{n}\right)}{v^{-\binom{n}{2}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}z^{n-1}}{v^{-\binom{n}{2}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{z^{n-1}}{v^{-\binom{n}{2}}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}u^{n}\frac{z^{n}}{v^{-\binom{n}{2}}v^{-n}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{sv,tv^{2}}!}=\exp_{s,t}(uvz,u)

and the proof is achieved.
∎

Corollary 3
.

From Eq.(
24
), the
q
-derivative of the deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function is

\displaystyle D_{q}(\exp_{s,t}(z,u))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(uz/\varphi_{s,t},u).

In particular

\displaystyle D_{q}(\exp_{s,t}(z))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(z/\varphi_{s,t}),

\displaystyle D_{q}(\exp_{s,t}^{\prime}(z))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}^{\prime}(-tz/\varphi_{s,t}),

\displaystyle D_{q}(\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z),

\displaystyle D_{q}(\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits^{\prime}_{s,t}(\varphi^{\prime}_{s,t}z/\varphi_{s,t}).

Corollary 4
.

From Eq.(
25
), the
U
-derivative of the deformed
(s,t)
-exponential functions is

\displaystyle D_{U}(\exp_{s,t}(z,u))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(iuz/\sqrt{t},u).

In particular

\displaystyle D_{U}(\exp_{s,t}(z))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(iz/\sqrt{t}),

\displaystyle D_{U}(\exp_{s,t}^{\prime}(z))

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(-i\sqrt{t}z),

\displaystyle D_{U}(\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(i\varphi_{s,t}z/\sqrt{t}),

\displaystyle D_{U}(\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z))

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits^{\prime}_{s,t}(i\varphi^{\prime}_{s,t}z/\sqrt{t}).

In the following results, we will give the infinite product representation of the functions
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)
and
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)
.

Theorem 13
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
t\neq 0
. If
|q|<1
, then the meromorphic continuation of
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)
is given by

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varphi_{s,t}^{k+1}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{k+1}-(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime k}z}.

If
|q|>1
, then
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)
is given by

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1-(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\frac{\varphi_{s,t}^{k}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(k+1)}}z\right).

Proof.

As
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}z)
, then

\frac{\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}z)-\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}z)}{(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})z}=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}z).

Suppose
|q|<1
. Then

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}z)

\displaystyle=[1-(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})z]^{-1}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}z)

and by setting
w=\varphi_{s,t}z
,

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(w)=(1-(1-q)w)^{-1}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(qw).

(34)

Now, suppose
|q|>1
. Then

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}z)=[1-(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})z]\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(\varphi_{s,t}z).

By setting
w=\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}z
,

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(w)=[1-(q^{-1}-1)w]\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(q^{-1}w).

(35)

Iterating Eqs. (
34
) and (
35
) yields desired results.
∎

Thus the meromorphic function
\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-(1-q)q^{k}z}
, with simple poles at
\frac{1}{(1-q)q^{k}}
,
k\in\mathbb{N}_{0}
, is a good substitute for
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)
.
The following are the specializations for the infinite product representation of the
(s,t)
-exponential function:

1.

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{1,1}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1+\sqrt{5})^{k+1}}{(1+\sqrt{5})^{k+1}-\sqrt{5}(1-\sqrt{5})^{k}z}.

2.

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{2,1}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(1+\sqrt{2})^{k+1}}{(1+\sqrt{2})^{k+1}-\sqrt{2}(1-\sqrt{2})^{k}z}.

3.

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{1,2}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{2^{k+1}}{2^{k+1}-3(-1)^{k}z}.

4.

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{3,-2}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1}{1-(z/2^{k+1})}.

5.

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{2t,-1}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{(t+\sqrt{t^{2}-1})^{k+1}}{(t+\sqrt{t^{2}-1})^{k+1}-\sqrt{t^{2}-1}(t-\sqrt{t^{2}-1})^{k}z}.

6.

\mathrm{e}_{p,q}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{p^{k+1}}{p^{k+1}-(p-q)q^{k}z}.

When
s\neq 0
and
t=0
, then

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,0}(z)=\frac{1}{1-z}.

Analogously, the following theorem is proved.

Theorem 14
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
t\neq 0
. If
|q|<1
, then

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\left(1+(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\frac{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime k}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{k+1}}z\right).

If
|q|>1
, then the meromorphic continuation of
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)
is given by

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(k+1)}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(k+1)}+(\varphi_{s,t}-\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\varphi_{s,t}^{k}z}.

From Theorem
13
and
14
we have the following result.

Corollary 5
.

Set
s\neq 0
. For all
q
with
q\neq 1
,

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(z)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(-z)=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(-z)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(z)=1.

We end this section by giving inequalities for the function
\exp_{s,t}(x,u)
.

Theorem 15
.

Set
s,t\in\mathbb{R}
such that
s\neq 0
and
s^{2}+4t>0
. The function
\exp_{s,t}(x,u)
satisfies the following inequalities:

1.

For
s,t
fixed and for
0<u<v
,
a\geq 0
, it holds

1\leq\exp_{s,t}(ax,u)\leq\exp_{s,t}(ax,v),\ 0\leq x<\infty.

2.

Fix
t\in\mathbb{R}
and tome
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
s_{1}<s_{2}
. Then

\exp_{s_{1},t}(x,u)>\exp_{s_{2},t}(x,u).

3.

Fix
s\in\mathbb{R}
and tome
t_{1},t_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
such that
t_{1}<t_{2}
. Then

\exp_{s,t_{1}}(x,u)>\exp_{s,t_{2}}(x,u).

Proof.

Since
0<u<v
and
a\geq 0
, we have for
n\geq 0

\displaystyle u^{\binom{n}{2}}

\displaystyle\leq v^{\binom{n}{2}}

\displaystyle u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

\displaystyle\leq v^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.

Taking summation over
n
, we get for
0\leq x<\infty
the desired result. Now set
t\in\mathbb{R}
and assume
s_{1}<s_{2}
for
s_{1},s_{2}\in\mathbb{R}
. Then
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}=s_{1}<s_{2}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}
. Assume by induction hypothesis that
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}<\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}
. In this way

\displaystyle\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}

\displaystyle=s_{1}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}+t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}

\displaystyle<s_{2}\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}+t\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n-1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}=\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}

and
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}<\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}
for all
n\geq 2
. It follows that
\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{1},t}!\leq\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s_{2},t}!
and that
\exp_{s_{1},t}(x,u)>\exp_{s_{2},t}(x,u)
. The last statement is proved in a similar way.
∎

Theorem 16
.

Set
s\neq 0
,
s^{2}+4t>0
.

1.

If
|q|<1
and
x\neq 0
, then

\exp_{s,t}(x,u)<\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(x)<e^{x},

(36)

provided that
u<\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}
. If
x<\frac{1}{1-q}
, then

e^{x}<\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(x).

2.

If
|q|>1
and
x\neq 0
, then

\exp_{s,t}(x,u)<\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(x)<e^{-x},

provided that
u<\varphi_{s,t}
. If
x<\frac{q}{q-1}
, then

e^{x}<\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(x).

Proof.

Set
|q|<1
. Denote

\displaystyle P_{n}(x)=\prod_{k=0}^{n}(1+x(1-q)q^{k}).

Then we have

P_{n}(x)<\exp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}x(1-q)q^{k}\right)=\exp\left(x(1-q^{n+1})\right),

hence

\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(x)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}P_{n}(x)<e^{x},\ \ x>0.

On the other side, if
x<\frac{1}{1-q}
,

\displaystyle\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(x)

\displaystyle=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\prod_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1}{1-(1-q)q^{k}z}

\displaystyle>\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}\exp\left(\sum_{k=0}^{n}(1-q)q^{k}x\right)

\displaystyle=\exp\left((1-q)x\frac{1}{1-q}\right)=e^{x}.

The remainder of Eq.(
36
) follows from Theorem
15
. The proof for
|q|>1
is similar to the previous one.
∎

The following result follows from Theorem
16
.

Corollary 6
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
s^{2}+4t>0
. Then

1.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\exp_{s,t}(-x,u)=0
, provided that
0<u<\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}
and
|q|<1
.

2.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(x)=\infty
, provided that
|q|<1
.

3.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(x)=\infty
, provided that
|q|>1
.

4.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(x)=\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\exp_{s,t}(x,u)=0
, provided that
|q|>1
and
0<u<\varphi_{s,t}
.

4
The equation
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)=f(x,y(x),y(ux))

4.1
Existence theorem for functional-difference equations with proportional delay

Definition 15
.

The equation

(\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y)(x)=f(x,y(x),y(ux)),\ y(0)=y_{0},

(37)

where
|f(x,y(x),y(ux))|x^{\alpha}
,
0\leq\alpha<1
, is bounded on some rectangle either

R_{\varphi}=\{|x|\leq a,|y(x)-y_{0}|\leq b/|\varphi_{s,t}|,|y(ux)-y_{0}|\leq b,a>0,\ b>0\},\hskip 14.22636pt0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|

or

R_{\varphi\prime}=\{|x|\leq a,|y(x)-y_{0}|\leq b/|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|,|y(ux)-y_{0}|\leq b,a>0,\ b>0\},\hskip 14.22636pt0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|,

will be called a functional-difference equation with proportional delay.

In the following results, our main objective is to prove the existence of solutions to the problem with initial value Eq.(
37
).

Theorem 17
.

A function
\phi
is a solution of Eq.(
37
) on an interval
I
if and only if it is a solution of the
(s,t)
-integral equation

y(x)=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,y(r),y(ur))d_{s,t}r

(38)

on
I
.

Proof.

Let
\phi
be a solution of the Eq.(
37
) on an interval
I
. Then

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}\phi(x)=f(x,\phi(x),\phi(ux)),\ \phi(0)=y_{0},

(39)

where either
0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|
or
0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
. From Theorem
8
, the equivalent
(s,t)
-integral equation to Eq.(
39
) is

\phi(x)=\phi(0)+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))d_{s,t}r

(40)

with
\phi(0)=y_{0}
. Thus
\phi
is a solution of Eq.(
38
). Conversely, suppose that Eq.(
40
) hold. By
(s,t)
-differentiate Eq.(
40
), we get

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}\phi(x)=f(x,\phi(x),\phi(ux))

where either
0<u<|\varphi_{s,t}|
or
0<u<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
, for all
x\in I
. From Eq.(
38
),
\phi(0)=y_{0}
. Hence
\phi
is a solution of Eq.(
37
).
∎

Theorem 18
.

Let
|f(x,y(x),y(ux))x^{\alpha}|
is bounded on
R_{\varphi}
for some
0\leq\alpha<1
and
|f|\leq M
on
R_{\varphi}
. The successive approximation

\phi_{0}(x)=y_{0},\hskip 14.22636pt\phi_{k+1}(x)=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))d_{s,t}r,\hskip 14.22636ptk=0,1,2,3,\ldots,

(41)

0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|
, exist on the interval
I_{\varphi}=[-\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}|},\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}|}]
, where
\alpha=\min\{a,\frac{b}{M}\}
. If
x\in I_{\varphi}
, then
(x,\phi_{k}(x),\phi_{k}(ux))\in R_{\varphi}
and
|\phi_{k}(x)-y_{0}|\leq M|x|
,
|\phi_{k}(ux)-y_{0}|\leq Mu|x|
.

Proof.

We prove the result by mathematical induction. Clearly
\phi_{0}(x)=y_{0}
is
(s,t)
-integrable on
I_{\varphi}
. Thus, the theorem is true for
k=0
. For
k=1
, we have

\displaystyle\phi_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{0}(r),\phi_{0}(ur))d_{s,t}r

\displaystyle=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,y_{0},y_{0})d_{s,t}r

and since
|f(x,\phi(x),\phi(ux))x^{\alpha}|
is bounded on
R_{\varphi}
, then
\phi_{1}(x)
exist. Also

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(x)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{0}(r),\phi_{0}(ur))d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{x}|f(r,y_{0},y_{0})|d_{s,t}r

\displaystyle\leq M|x|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{|\varphi_{s,t}|},\hskip 14.22636ptx\in I_{\varphi}

and

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(ux)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{ux}f(r,\phi_{0}(r),\phi_{0}(ur))d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{ux}|f(r,y_{0},y_{0})|d_{s,t}r

\displaystyle\leq Mu|x|,\hskip 14.22636pt0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|

\displaystyle\leq b,\ x\in I_{\varphi}.

Thus, for
x\in I_{\varphi}
,
(x,\phi_{1}(x),\phi_{1}(ux))\in R_{\varphi}
and
|\phi_{1}(x)-y_{0}|\leq M|x|
,
|\phi_{1}(ux)-y_{0}|\leq Mu|x|
. The theorem is true for
k=1
. Assume that theorem is true for
k=n
, i.e., for
x\in I_{\varphi}
,
(x,\phi_{n}(x),\phi_{n}(ux))\in R
and
|\phi_{n}(x)-y_{0}|\leq M|x|
,
|\phi_{n}(ux)-y_{0}|\leq Mu|x|
. For
k=n+1

\phi_{n+1}(x)=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{n}(r),\phi_{n}(ur))d_{s,t}r.

Since
|f(x,\phi_{n}(x),\phi_{n}(ux))x^{\alpha}|
is bounded on
R_{\varphi}
,
0\leq\alpha<1
, then
\phi_{n+1}(x)
exists on
I_{\varphi}
. Also,

\displaystyle|\phi_{n+1}(x)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq M|x|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{b}{|\varphi_{s,t}|},\hskip 14.22636ptx\in I_{\varphi}

and

\displaystyle|\phi_{n+1}(ux)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq Mu|x|

\displaystyle\leq M|\varphi_{s,t}||x|,\hskip 14.22636pt0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|

\displaystyle\leq b,\hskip 14.22636ptx\in I_{\varphi}.

Thus, if
x\in I_{\varphi}
,
(x,\phi_{n+1}(x),\phi_{n+1}(ux))\in R_{\varphi}
and
|\phi_{n+1}(x)-y_{0}|\leq M|x|
,
|\phi_{n+1}(ux)|\leq Mu|x|
.
∎

Theorem 19
.

Let
|f(x,y(x),y(ux))x^{\alpha}|
is bounded on
R_{\varphi^{\prime}}
for some
0\leq\alpha<1
and
|f|\leq M
on
R_{\varphi^{\prime}}
. The successive approximation

\phi_{0}(x)=y_{0},\hskip 14.22636pt\phi_{k+1}(x)=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))d_{s,t}r,\hskip 14.22636ptk=0,1,2,3,\ldots,

0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
, exist on the interval
I_{\varphi^{\prime}}=[-\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|},\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|}]
, where
\alpha=\min\{a,\frac{b}{M}\}
. If
x\in I_{\varphi^{\prime}}
, then
(x,\phi_{k}(x),\phi_{k}(ux))\in R_{\varphi^{\prime}}
and
|\phi_{k}(x)-y_{0}|\leq M|x|
,
|\phi_{k}(ux)-y_{0}|\leq Mu|x|
.

Theorem 20

(
Existence Theorem 1).

Let
|f(x,y(x),y(ux))x^{\alpha}|
be a function bounded on
R_{\varphi}
,
0\leq\alpha<1
, with
|f|\leq M
on
R_{\varphi}
. Further, suppose
f
satisfies the Lipschitz condition in the second and third variables with Lipschitz constant
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
such that

|f(x,y_{1}(x),y_{1}(ux))-f(x,y_{2}(x),y_{2}(ux))|\leq L_{1}|y_{1}(x)-y_{2}(x)|+L_{2}|y_{1}(ux)-y_{2}(ux)|.

Then the successive approximations Eq.(
41
) converge on the interval
I_{\varphi}=[-\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}|},\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}|}]
, where
\alpha=\min\{a,b/M\}
, to a solution
\phi
of the initial value problem

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=f(x,y(x),y(ux)),\hskip 14.22636pty(0)=y_{0}

(42)

on
I_{\varphi}
when
0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}|
and
(q,\varphi_{s,t})
belongs to one of the sets
S_{1},S_{2},S_{3},S_{6},S_{7},S_{8},S_{9}
of the Theorem
30
and
I_{\varphi}
is contained in one the intervals

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{L_{1}|1-q|},\frac{1}{L_{1}|1-q|}\right),\ \left(-\frac{1}{L_{2}|1-q|},\frac{1}{L_{2}|1-q|}\right),

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{(L_{1}+L_{2}))|1-q|},\frac{1}{(L_{1}+L_{2}))|1-q|}\right).

Proof.

Let’s note that
\phi_{n}
can be written as

\phi_{n}=\phi_{0}+(\phi_{1}-\phi_{0})+(\phi_{2}-\phi_{1})+\cdots+(\phi_{n}-\phi_{n-1}),

and hence
\phi_{n}(x)
is a partial sum for the series

\phi_{0}+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}[\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)].

(43)

Therefore to show that the sequence
\{\phi_{n}(x)\}
converges is equivalent to showing that the series of the Eq.(
43
) converges. To prove the latter we must estimate the terms
\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)
of this series. We have that

\displaystyle|\phi_{1}(x)-\phi_{0}(x)|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}f(r,y_{0},y_{0})d_{s,t}r\Bigg|\leq\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}|f(r,y_{0},y_{0})|d_{s,t}r\Bigg|\leq M|x|.

(44)

Writing down the relations defining
\phi_{2}
and
\phi_{1}
, and subtracting, we obtain

\phi_{2}(x)-\phi_{1}(x)=\int_{0}^{x}[f(r,\phi_{1}(r),\phi_{1}(ux))-f(r,\phi_{0}(r),\phi_{0}(ur))]d_{s,t}r.

Therefore

\displaystyle|\phi_{2}(x)-\phi_{1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}|f(r,\phi_{1}(r),\phi_{1}(ux))-f(r,\phi_{0}(r),\phi_{0}(ur))|d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}[L_{1}|\phi_{1}(r)-\phi_{0}(r)|+L_{2}|\phi_{1}(ur)-\phi_{0}(ur)|]d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq M(L_{1}+L_{2}u)\Bigg|\frac{x^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq M(L_{1}+L_{2}u)\frac{|x|^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}}.

It can also be shown that

|\phi_{3}(x)-\phi_{2}(x)|\leq M(L_{1}+L_{2}u)(L_{1}+L_{2}u^{2})\frac{|x|^{3}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{3}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

and

|\phi_{4}(x)-\phi_{3}(x)|\leq M(L_{1}+L_{2}u)(L_{1}+L_{2}u^{2})(L_{1}+L_{2}u^{3})\frac{|x|^{4}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{4}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}.

In general, it is easy to show by induction for
n\geq 1
that

\displaystyle|\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq M\prod_{k=1}^{n-1}(L_{1}+L_{2}u^{k})\frac{|x|^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}=\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{n}\frac{|x|^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

(45)

where we have used Proposition
2
. Then

\displaystyle|\phi_{n}(x)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\sum_{k=1}^{n}[\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k-1}(x)]\Bigg|\leq\sum_{k=1}^{n}|\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k-1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{k}\frac{|x|^{k}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{k}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{k}\frac{|x|^{k}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{k}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}(\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2},u;|x|)-1).

From Theorem
30
, the power series for
\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2},u;|x|)
is convergent, then the series

|y_{0}|+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}|\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)|

is convergent on
I_{\varphi}
. This implies that the series (
43
) is convergent on
I_{\varphi}
. Therefore the
n
-th partial sum of Eq.(
43
), which is just
\phi_{n}(x)
, tends to a limits
\phi(x)
as
n\rightarrow\infty
, for each
x\in I_{\varphi}
.

This limit function
\phi
is a solution to our problem on
I_{\varphi}
. First, let us show that
\phi
is continuous on
I_{\varphi}
. This may be seen in the following way. If
x_{1},x_{2}\in I

\displaystyle|\phi_{n+1}(x_{1})-\phi_{n+1}(x_{2})|=\Bigg|\int_{x_{1}}^{x_{2}}f(r,\phi_{n}(r),\phi_{n}(ur))d_{s,t}r\Bigg|\leq M|x_{1}-x_{2}|,

which implies, by letting
n\rightarrow\infty
,

|\phi(x_{1})-\phi(x_{2})|\leq M|x_{1}-x_{2}|.

(46)

Then
\phi(x)
is continuous on
I_{\varphi}
. Also, by setting
x_{1}=x
,
x_{2}=0
in Eq.(
46
) we obtain

\displaystyle|\phi(x)-y_{0}|

\displaystyle\leq M|x|\hskip 14.22636pt\text{and}\hskip 14.22636pt|\phi(ux)-y_{0}|\leq Mu|x|

which implies that the points
(x,\phi(x),\phi(ux))
are in
R_{\varphi}
for all
x\in I_{\varphi}
. We now estimate
