|\phi(x)-\phi_{p}(x)|
. We have

\phi(x)=\phi_{0}(x)+\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}[\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k-1}(x)],

and

\phi_{p}(x)=\phi_{0}(x)+\sum_{k=1}^{p}[\phi_{k}(x)-\phi_{k-1}(x)].

Therefore, using Eq.(
45
), we find that

\displaystyle|\phi(x)-\phi_{p}(x)|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\sum_{n=p+1}^{\infty}[\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)]\Bigg|

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{n=p+1}^{\infty}|\phi_{n}(x)-\phi_{n-1}(x)|

\displaystyle\leq\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sum_{n=p+1}^{\infty}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{n}\frac{a^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

\displaystyle=\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{n+p+1}\frac{a^{n+p+1}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+p+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{Ma^{p+1}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{p+1}}{(L_{1}+L_{2})\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{p+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2}u^{p+1})_{1,u}^{n}\frac{a^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}

\displaystyle=\frac{Ma^{p+1}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{p+1}}{(L_{1}+L_{2})\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{p+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2}u^{p+1},u;a)

(47)

Letting
\sigma_{p}=a^{p+1}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{p+1}/\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{p+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!
, we see that
\sigma_{p}\rightarrow 0
as
p\rightarrow\infty
, since
\sigma_{p}
is a general term for the series for
\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2},u;a)
. In terms of
\sigma_{p}
Eq.(
47
) may be written as

|\phi(x)-\phi_{p}(x)|\leq\frac{M}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sigma_{p}\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2}u^{p+1},u;a).

(48)

To complete the proof we must show that

\phi(x)=y_{0}+\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))d_{s,t}r

(49)

for all
x\in I_{\varphi}
. For this purpose, we will show that

\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))d_{s,t}r\rightarrow\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))d_{s,t}r

(50)

for all
x
in
R_{\varphi}
as
k\rightarrow\infty
. We have

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))d_{s,t}r-\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}|f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))-f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))|d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 85.35826pt\leq L_{1}\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}|\phi(r)-\phi_{k}(r)|d_{s,t}r\Bigg|+L_{2}\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}|\phi(ur)-\phi_{k}(ur)|d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

(51)

using the fact that
f
satisfies a Lipschitz condition. The estimate Eq.(
48
) can now used in Eq.(
51
) to obtain

\displaystyle\Bigg|\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi(r),\phi(ur))d_{s,t}r-\int_{0}^{x}f(r,\phi_{k}(r),\phi_{k}(ur))d_{s,t}r\Bigg|

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt\leq\frac{M(L_{1}+L_{2})}{L_{1}+L_{2}}\sigma_{k}\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2}u^{k+1},u;a)|x|=M\sigma_{k}\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2}u^{k+1},u;a)|x|

which tends to zero as
k\rightarrow\infty
, for each
x
in
I_{\varphi}
. This proves Eq.(
50
), and hence that satisfies Eq.(
49
). Thus our proof of theorem is now complete.
∎

The estimate Eq.(
47
) of how well the
p
-th approximation
\phi_{p}
approximates the solution
\phi
is worthy of special attention.

Theorem 21
.

The
p
-th successive approximation
\phi_{p}
to the solution
\phi
of the initial value problem of Theorem
20
satisfies

\displaystyle|\phi(x)-\phi_{p}(x)|\leq\frac{Ma^{p+1}(L_{1}\oplus L_{2})_{1,u}^{p+1}}{(L_{1}+L_{2})\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{p+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{|s|,t}!}\mathrm{E}_{|s|,t}(L_{1},L_{2}u^{p+1},u;a)

for all
x
in
I_{\varphi}
.

Theorem 22

(
Existence Theorem 2).

Let
|f(x,y(x),y(ux))x^{\alpha}|
be a function bounded on
R_{\varphi^{\prime}}
,
0\leq\alpha<1
, with
|f|\leq M
on
R_{\varphi^{\prime}}
. Further, suppose
f
satisfies the Lipschitz condition in the second and third variables with Lipschitz constant
L_{1}
and
L_{2}
such that

|f(x,y_{1}(x),y_{1}(ux))-f(x,y_{2}(x),y_{2}(ux))|\leq L_{1}|y_{1}(x)-y_{2}(x)|+L_{2}|y_{1}(ux)-y_{2}(ux)|.

Then the successive approximations Eq.(
41
) converge on the interval
I_{\varphi^{\prime}}=[-\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|},\frac{\alpha}{|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|}]
, where
\alpha=\min\{a,b/M\}
, to a solution
\phi
of the initial value problem

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=f(x,y(x),y(ux)),\hskip 14.22636pty(0)=y_{0}

(52)

on
I_{\varphi^{\prime}}
when
0<u\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
and
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})
belongs to one of the sets
T_{1},T_{2},T_{3},T_{6},T_{7},T_{8},T_{9}
of the Theorem
31
and
I_{\varphi^{\prime}}
is contained in one the intervals

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{L_{1}|q^{-1}-1|},\frac{1}{L_{1}|q^{-1}-1|}\right),\ \left(-\frac{1}{L_{2}|q^{-1}-1|},\frac{1}{L_{2}|q^{-1}-1|}\right),

\displaystyle\left(-\frac{1}{(L_{1}+L_{2}))|q^{-1}-1|},\frac{1}{(L_{1}+L_{2}))|q^{-1}-1|}\right).

4.2
Solutions of equation
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=f(y(ux))
via Bell polynomials

The differential equation
y^{\prime}=f(y)
is studied in
[
41
,
42
,
43
,
44
]
. It is a known fact that Bell polynomials are closely related to the derivatives of the compositions of functions
[
45
]
,
[
46
]
,
[
47
]
. Let
f
and
g
be functions with exponential generating functions
\sum a_{n}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}
and
\sum b_{n}\frac{x^{n}}{n!}

respectively, with
a_{n},b_{n}\in\mathbb{C}
. Then

f(g(x))=f(b_{0})+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}(b_{0})B_{n,k}(b_{1},...,b_{n-k+1})\frac{x^{n}}{n!}

(53)

where
B_{n,k}(b_{1},...,b_{n-k+1})
is the
n
-th partial Bell polynomial

B_{n,k}(b_{1},...,b_{n-k+1})=\sum_{|p(n)|=k}\frac{n!}{j_{1}!j_{2}!\cdots j_{n}!}\left[\frac{b_{1}}{1!}\right]^{j_{1}}\left[\frac{b_{2}}{2!}\right]^{j_{2}}\cdots\left[\frac{b_{n}}{n!}\right]^{j_{n}}

and the sum runs over all partitions
p(n)
of
n
, that is,
n=j_{1}+2j_{2}+\cdots+nj_{n}
,
j_{h}
denotes the number of parts of size
h
and
|p(n)|=j_{1}+j_{2}+\cdots+j_{n}
is the length
of the partition
p(n)
. All the above together with the identity

B_{n,k}(abx_{1},ab^{2}x_{2},...,ab^{n-k+1}x_{n-k+1})=a^{k}b^{n}B_{n,k}(x_{1},x_{2},...,x_{n-k+1})

(54)

allows us to obtain the functional-difference equation with proportional delay
(s,t)
-analog of
y^{\prime}=f(y)
.

Theorem 23
.

Suppose
f
with representation in power series. Then the series

y(x)=y_{0}+f\left(y_{0}\right)x+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(y_{0}\right)B_{n,k}\left(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-k+1}\right)\frac{x^{n+1}}{n!},

where the
y_{i}
are in terms of
y_{0}
, is solution of the
autonomous equation with proportional delay

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=f(y(ux)),\ y(0)=y_{0}.

Corollary 7
.

Suppose
f
with representation in power series. Then the series

y(x)=y_{0}+f\left(y_{0}\right)x+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}u^{n}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(y_{0}\right)B_{n,k}\left(y_{1},\ldots,y_{n-k+1}\right)\frac{x^{n+1}}{(n+1)!},

where the
y_{i}
are in terms of
y_{0}
, is solution of the
autonomous equation with proportional delay

y^{\prime}=f(y(ux)),\ y(0)=y_{0}.

In the following result, we show that there is an infinite number of solutions to a functional difference equation with proportional delay if
x
is taken to be positive.

Theorem 24
.

Suppose
f
with representation in power series. Take
p(x)=G(\log_{q}(x))
,
x>0
, in
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
. The series

y(x)=p(x)+f\left(p\left(\frac{u}{\varphi_{s,t}}x\right)\right)x\\
+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(p\left(\frac{u}{\varphi_{s,t}}x\right)\right)B_{n,k}\left(p_{1}\left(\frac{u}{\varphi_{s,t}}x\right),\ldots,p_{n-k+1}\left(\frac{u}{\varphi_{s,t}}x\right)\right)\frac{x^{n+1}}{n!},

(55)

where the
p_{i}(x)
are functions in
p(x)
, is solution of the
autonomous equation with proportional delay

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=f(y(ux)).

(56)

This means that there are an infinite number of solutions to Eq.(
56
).

Proof.

Let
(p_{n}(x))_{n\in\mathbb{N}_{0}}
be a sequence of functions in
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
and suppose that
y(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p_{n}(x)x^{n}/n!
is solution of Eq.(
56
). Then Eq.(
56
) is equivalent to

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}p_{n+1}(\varphi_{s,t}x)\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}\frac{x^{n}}{(n+1)!}\\
=f(p_{0}(ux))+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}(p_{0}(ux))B_{n,k}(up_{1}(ux),\ldots,u^{n-k+1}p_{n-k+1}(ux))\frac{x^{n}}{n!}

and if set
p_{0}(x)=p(x)
, then it follows that

\displaystyle p_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=f\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right),

(57)

\displaystyle p_{n+1}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{n+1}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 42.67912pt\times B_{n,k}\left(up_{1}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right),\ldots,u^{n-k+1}p_{n-k+1}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=\frac{(n+1)u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)B_{n,k}\left(p_{1}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right),\ldots,p_{n-k+1}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)

(58)

where we have used Eq.(
54
). Taking
(s,t)
-derivative to Eq.(
55
), we have

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)

\displaystyle=f(p(ux))

\displaystyle+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\frac{u^{n}}{n!\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(p\left(ux\right)\right)B_{n,k}\left(p_{1}\left(ux\right),\ldots,p_{n-k+1}\left(ux\right)\right)\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}x^{n}

\displaystyle=f(p(ux))+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\sum_{k=1}^{n}f^{(k)}\left(p\left(ux\right)\right)B_{n,k}\left(p_{1}\left(ux\right),\ldots,p_{n-k+1}\left(ux\right)\right)\frac{(ux^{n})}{n!}

\displaystyle=f(y(ux))

which completes the proof.
∎

Some values of
p_{i}(x)
are

\displaystyle p_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=f\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right),

\displaystyle p_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{u}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}f^{\prime}\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f\left(p\left(\frac{u^{2}x}{\varphi_{s,t}^{2}}\right)\right),

\displaystyle p_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=\frac{u^{3}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{3}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}f^{\prime}\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f^{\prime}\left(p\left(\frac{u^{2}x}{\varphi_{s,t}^{2}}\right)\right)f\left(p\left(\frac{u^{3}x}{\varphi_{s,t}^{3}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\hskip 56.9055pt+\frac{u^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{3}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}f^{\prime\prime}\left(p\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)f^{2}\left(p\left(\frac{u^{2}x}{\varphi_{s,t}^{2}}\right)\right).

4.3
Solution of equation
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)=ay(ux)

Theorem 25
.

Set
s\neq 0
,
t\neq 0
.

1.

A solution to the initial value problem

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)

\displaystyle=ay(ux),

(59)

\displaystyle y(0)

\displaystyle=\xi

is

y(x)=\xi\exp_{s,t}(ax,u)=\xi\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.

2.

For
x>0
, the solutions of Eq.(
59
) are of the form

y(x)=\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,x,u,p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}p\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}x\right)\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

(60)

where
p(x)=G(\log_{q}(x))
, with
G
a periodic function with period one.

Proof.

From Theorem
8
,

y(x)=\xi+\int_{0}^{x}y(ux)d_{s,t}x.

By successive approximation method define

\displaystyle y_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi,

\displaystyle y_{k+1}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi+\int_{0}^{x}y_{k}(ur)d_{s,t}r.

Then

\displaystyle y_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi,

\displaystyle y_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi+a\xi x,

\displaystyle y_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi+a\xi x+\xi u\frac{a^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}x^{2},

\displaystyle y_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi+a\xi x+\xi u\frac{a^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}x^{2}+\xi u^{3}\frac{a^{3}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{3}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}x^{3},

\displaystyle y_{k}(x)

\displaystyle=\xi\sum_{n=0}^{k}a^{n}u^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

and

y(x)=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}y_{k}(x)=\xi\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}=\xi\exp_{s,t}(ax,u).

From Eqs. (
57
) and (
58
) with
f(x)=ax
, we have

\displaystyle p_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=ap\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right),

\displaystyle p_{n+1}(x)

\displaystyle=a\frac{(n+1)u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}B_{n,1}\left(p_{1}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right),\ldots,p_{n}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle=a\frac{(n+1)u^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}p_{n}\left(\frac{ux}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right).

Therefore

p_{n}(x)=\frac{a^{n}n!u^{\binom{n}{2}}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}p\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}x\right)

and

y(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\frac{a^{n}u^{\binom{n}{2}}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}p\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}x\right)x^{n}

is a solution of Eq.(
59
).
∎

As

G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}z\right)\right)=G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime n}}z\right)\right)

for all periodic function
G
with period one and all
n\in\mathbb{N}
, then

\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}G\left(\log_{q}\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime n}}z\right)\right)\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

are also solutions of Eq.(
59
).

The
(p,q)
-power is defined by Sadjang in
[
35
]
by

(x\ominus a)_{p,q}^{n}=\begin{cases}1,&\text{ if }n=0;\\
\prod_{k=0}^{n-1}(p^{k}x-q^{k}a),&\text{ if }n\geq 1.\end{cases}

(61)

In
[
36
]
the following expansion is obtained

(x\ominus a)_{p,q}^{n}=\sum_{k=0}^{n}\genfrac{}{}{0.0pt}{}{n}{k}_{p,q}p^{\binom{k}{2}}q^{\binom{n-k}{2}}x^{k}(-a)^{n-k}.

(62)

Definition 16
.

For all
s\neq 0
,
s^{2}+4t>0
, we define the binomial
(s,t)
-exponential function as

\displaystyle\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus y)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}a^{n}\frac{(x\ominus y)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}=\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(ax)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(-ay).

Theorem 26
.

Let
a
be a complex number and set
s\neq 0
,
s^{2}+4t>0
. Then the initial value problem

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)=ay(\varphi_{s,t}x),

(63)

y(0)=\xi
, have solution

f(x)=\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(ax).

A solution of Eq.(
63
) with initial value
y(\eta)=\xi
is

f(x)=\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(ax)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(-a\eta)=\xi\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus\eta)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}).

Also, if
f(x)=\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus\eta)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}})
is a solution of Eq.(
63
) with
f(\eta)=1
,
\eta>0
, then
g(x)=G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)
is also a solution for every
G(\log_{q}(x))
in
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
and
g(\eta)=G(\log_{q}(\eta))
.

Proof.

The solution follows from Theorem
25
with
u=\varphi_{s,t}
. As

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(\xi\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus\eta)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}})

\displaystyle=\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(-a\eta)\mathbf{D}_{s,t}\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(ax)

\displaystyle=a\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(-a\eta)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(a\varphi_{s,t}x)

\displaystyle=a\xi\exp_{s,t}(a(\varphi_{s,t}x\ominus\eta)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}),

then
f(x)=\xi\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus\eta))
is solution of Eq.(
63
) with initial value
f(\eta)=\xi
. Suppose
f(x)=\exp_{s,t}(a(x\ominus\eta))
is a solution of Eq.(
63
). Then

\displaystyle\mathbf{D}_{s,t}(G(\log_{q}(x))f(x))

\displaystyle=G(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))\mathbf{D}_{s,t}f(x)

\displaystyle=aG(\log_{q}(\varphi_{s,t}x))f(\varphi_{s,t}x)

and
G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)
is also a solution for all
\eta>0
and for all functions
G(\log_{q}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
.
∎

Similarly, we have the following theorem.

Theorem 27
.

Let
a
be a complex number and set
s\neq 0
,
s^{2}+4t>0
. Then the initial value problem

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)=ay(\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}x),

(64)

y(0)=\xi
, have solution

f(x)=\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(ax).

A solution of Eq.(
63
) with initial value
y(\eta)=\xi
is

f(x)=\xi\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(ax)\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(-a\eta)=\xi\exp_{s,t}(a((-\eta)\oplus x)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}}).

Also, if
f(x)=\exp_{s,t}(a((-\eta)\oplus x)_{\varphi,\varphi^{\prime}})
is a solution of Eq.(
63
) with
f(\eta)=1
,
\eta>0
, then
g(x)=G(\log_{q}(x))f(x)
is also a solution for every
G(\log_{q}(x))
in
\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
and
g(\eta)=G(\log_{q}(\eta))
.

Next, we will give some properties of the functions
\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p)
.

Proposition 6
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
s^{2}+4t>0
. For all
a,u\in\mathbb{R}
and
p(x)=f(\log_{q}(z)),q(x)=g(\log_{q}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
. Then

1.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,0)=0
.

2.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,c)=c\exp_{s,t}(az,u)
.

3.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,\varphi_{s,t},p)=f(\log_{q}(z))\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(az)
.

4.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime},p)=f(\log_{q}(z))\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(az)
.

5.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p+q)=\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p)+\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,q)
.

6.

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,q^{-m}u,p)=e_{q^{m}s,q^{2m}t}(a,z,u,p)
, for all
m\in\mathbb{Z}
.

Proof.

By direct application of Eq. (
60
).
∎

From the previous proposition it follows that the function
\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p)
generalizes to the
(s,t)
-exponential functions
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}(az,u)
and
\mathop{\mbox{{Exp}}}\nolimits_{s,t}^{\prime}(az,u)
. In the following theorems, elementary analytic properties of
\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p)
are shown.

Theorem 28
.

Take
p(x)=f(\log_{q}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
such that
f(x)
is a continuous periodic function with period one with
|f(x)|\leq M
for all
x\in\mathbb{R}
. Then

\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,x,u,p)\leq M\exp_{s,t}(ax,u)

Proof.

Since
f
is continuous and periodical, then there exists a number
M>0
such that
f
is bounded with
|p(x)|<M
for all
z
. Then

\displaystyle\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,z,u,p)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}p\left(\frac{u^{n}}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}x\right)\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

\displaystyle\leq M\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}u^{\binom{n}{2}}a^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

\displaystyle=M\exp_{s,t}(ax,u)

and
\exp_{s,t}(ax,u)
is an upper bound of
\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,x,u,p)
.
∎

Corollary 8
.

Set
s\neq 0
and
s^{2}+4t>0
and take
p(x)=f(\log_{q}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{s,t}
such that
|f(x)|\leq M
for all
x>0
. Then

1.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,x,u,p)=\infty
provided that
u>0
.

2.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,-x,u,p)=0
, provided that
|q|>1
and
u<\varphi_{s,t}
.

3.

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{e}_{s,t}(a,-x,u,p)=0
, provided that
|q|<1
and
u<\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}
.

Example 4
.

Find the solution to the functional equation

f(3x)-f(2x)=xf(x/2),\ f(0)=1.

(65)

We have that
\varphi_{s,t}=3
and
\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}=2
, with
s=5
and
t=-6
. Therefore, the solution is

f(x)=\exp_{5,-6}(x,1/2)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\binom{n}{2}}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{5,-6}!},

convergent in
\mathbb{R}
, where

\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{5,-6}=(0,1,5,19,65,211,\ldots).

The Eq.(
65
) with initial value
f(\eta)=\xi
,
\eta>0
have solutions

f(x)=\mathrm{e}_{5,-6}(1,x,1/2,p)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}\left(\frac{1}{2}\right)^{\binom{n}{2}}G\left(\log_{2/3}\left(\frac{x}{6^{n}}\right)\right)\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{5,-6}!},

for each
p(x)=G(\log_{2/3}(x))\in\mathbb{P}_{5,-6}
. If
G(x)=\sin(2\pi x)
, then

\mathrm{e}_{5,-6}(1,x,1/2,p)\leq\exp_{5,-6}(x,1/2)

and
\mathrm{e}_{5,-6}(1,x,1/2,p)
is convergent in
\mathbb{R}
, too. Also,

\lim_{x\rightarrow\infty}\mathrm{e}_{5,-6}(1,-x,1/2,p)=0.

4.4
Solution of
\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=ay(x)+by(ux)

Theorem 29
.

The Pantograph equation

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y=ay(x)+by(ux),\ y(0)=1

has solution

y(x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(a\oplus b)_{1,u}^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

Proof.

By successive approximation method, we obtain

\displaystyle y_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle y_{k+1}(x)

\displaystyle=1+\int_{0}^{x}(ay_{k}(r)+by(ur))d_{s,t}r.

Then

\displaystyle y_{0}(x)

\displaystyle=1,

\displaystyle y_{1}(x)

\displaystyle=1+(a+b)x,

\displaystyle y_{2}(x)

\displaystyle=1+(a+b)x+(a+b)(a+bu)\frac{x^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!},

\displaystyle y_{3}(x)

\displaystyle=1+(a+b)x+(a+b)(a+bu)\frac{x^{2}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{2}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

\displaystyle\hskip 28.45274pt+(a+b)(a+bu)(a+bu^{2})\frac{x^{3}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{3}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.

In general

y_{k}(x)=1+\sum_{n=1}^{k}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(a+bu^{i})\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}

and

y(x)=\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}y_{k}(x)=1+\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(a+bu^{i})\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.

∎

Definition 17
.

We define the
(1,u)
-deformed
(s,t)
-exponential function as

\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(a\oplus b)_{1,u}^{n}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.

When
u=q
, we define
\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;z)=\mathrm{E}_{1+q,-q}(a,b,q;z)
.

Some special values of the function
\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;x)
are

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,0,-;x)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(ax),

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(0,a,u;x)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}(ax,u),

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,1;x)

\displaystyle=\exp_{s,t}((a+b)x),

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(ac,bc,u;x)

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;cx).

Theorem 30
.

Suppose that
a\neq 0
and
b\neq 0
. If
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}|
, then the function
\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;z)
is

1.

entire only if either
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{1}
or
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{2}
,

S_{1}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):|q|\neq 1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|>1\},\ S_{2}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):|q|>1,\ 1\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}|<|q|\}

2.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|1-q||a|}
only if
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{3}
,

S_{3}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):0<|q|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|=1\},

3.

convergent in
z=0
if either
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{4}
or
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{5}
,

S_{4}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):0<|q|<1,\ 0<|\varphi_{s,t}|<1\},\ S_{5}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):|q|>1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{-1}|>|q|\}.

If
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}|
, then the function
\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;z)
is

1.

entire only if either
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{6}
or
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{7}
,
a+b=0
,

S_{6}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):|q|>1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|>1\},\ S_{7}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):0<|q|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|=1\},

2.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|b||1-q|}
only if
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{8}
,

S_{8}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):0<|q|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|>1\},

3.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|a+b||1-q|}
only if
(q,\varphi_{s,t})\in S_{9}
,
a+b\neq 0
,

S_{9}=\{(q,\varphi_{s,t}):0<|q|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}|=1\}.

Proof.

Define
a_{n}=(a\oplus b)_{1,u}^{n}\frac{z^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}
. Then

\displaystyle\Big|\frac{a_{n+1}}{a_{n}}\Big|

\displaystyle=\Bigg|\frac{(a\oplus b)_{1,u}^{n+1}z^{n+1}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}\cdot\frac{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}{(a\oplus b)_{1,u}^{n}z^{n}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|z|\Bigg|\frac{a+bu^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n+1}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}}\Bigg|

\displaystyle=|z||1-q|\Bigg|\frac{a}{\varphi_{s,t}^{n}}+b\left(\frac{u}{\varphi_{s,t}}\right)^{n}\Bigg|\Bigg|\frac{1}{1-q^{n+1}}\Bigg|.

Now, apply each condition for
q
and
\varphi_{s,t}
.
∎

Theorem 31
.

Suppose that
a\neq 0
and
b\neq 0
. If
|u|<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
, then the function
\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;z)
is

1.

entire only if either
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{1}
or
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{2}
,

\displaystyle T_{1}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):|q^{-1}|\neq 1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|>1\},

\displaystyle T_{2}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):|q^{-1}|>1,\ 1\leq|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(-1)}|<|q^{-1}|\},

2.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|q^{-1}-1||a|}
only if
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{3}
,

T_{3}=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):0<|q^{-1}|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|=1\},

3.

convergent in
z=0
if either
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{4}
or
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{5}
,

\displaystyle T_{4}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):0<|q^{-1}|<1,\ 0<|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|<1\},

\displaystyle T_{5}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):|q^{-1}|>1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime(-1)}|>|q^{-1}|\}.

If
|u|=|\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|
, then the function
\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(a,b,u;z)
is

1.

entire only if either
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{6}
or
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{7}
,
a+b=0
,

\displaystyle T_{6}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):|q^{-1}|>1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|>1\},

\displaystyle T_{7}

\displaystyle=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):0<|q^{-1}|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|=1\},

2.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|b||q^{-1}-1|}
only if
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{8}
,

T_{8}=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):0<|q^{-1}|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|>1\},

3.

convergent in
|z|<\frac{1}{|a+b||q^{-1}-1|}
only if
(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime})\in T_{9}
,
a+b\neq 0
,

T_{9}=\{(q^{-1},\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}):0<|q^{-1}|<1,\ |\varphi_{s,t}^{\prime}|=1\}.

Theorem 32
.

For
0<|q|<1

\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1+b(1-q)q^{k}z}{1-a(1-q)q^{k}z}=\frac{\mathop{\mbox{{E}}}\nolimits_{1/q}(bz)}{\mathop{\mbox{{E}}}\nolimits_{1/q}(-az)}

where
\mathop{\mbox{{E}}}\nolimits_{1/q}(z)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{\binom{n}{2}}z^{n}/[n]_{q}!=\prod_{n=0}^{\infty}(1+(1-q)q^{n}z)
.
For
|q|>1

\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;z)=\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1-a(q^{-1}-1)q^{-k}z}{1+b(q^{-1}-1)q^{-k}z}.

Proof.

Suppose
0<|q|<1
and write
y(x)=\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;x)
. As
D_{q}y(x)=ay(x)+by(qx)
, then

y(x)-y(qx)=a(1-q)xy(x)+b(1-q)xy(qx)

and

y(x)=y(qx)\frac{1+b(1-q)x}{1-a(1-q)x}.

(66)

Iterating Eq.(
66
) we get

y(x)=y(q^{n+1}x)\prod_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1+b(1-q)q^{k}x}{1-a(1-q)q^{k}x}

and therefore

y(x)=\lim_{n\rightarrow\infty}y(q^{n+1}x)\prod_{k=0}^{n}\frac{1+b(1-q)q^{k}x}{1-a(1-q)q^{k}x}=y(0)\prod_{k=0}^{\infty}\frac{1+b(1-q)q^{k}x}{1-a(1-q)q^{k}x}.

The proof for
|q|>1
is similar.
∎

Proposition 7
.

For all
a,b\in\mathbb{C}

1.

\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;z)\mathrm{E}_{q}(b,a;-z)=1
.

2.

\mathrm{E}_{q^{-1}}(a,b;z)=\mathrm{E}_{q}(b,a;z)
.

3.

\mathrm{E}_{q}(1,-q;z)=\frac{1}{1-(1-q)z}
.

4.

\mathrm{E}_{q}(-q,1;z)=1+(1-q)z
.

Proof.

To prove 1. we use the Definition
17
and Theorem
32

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q}(a,b;z)\mathrm{E}_{q}(b,a;-z)

\displaystyle=\frac{E_{1/q}(bz)}{E_{1/q}(-az)}\cdot\frac{E_{1/q}(-az)}{E_{1/q}(bz)}=1.

As
(a\oplus b)_{1,q^{-1}}^{n}=q^{-\binom{n}{2}}(b\oplus a)_{1,q}^{n}
, then

\displaystyle\mathrm{E}_{q^{-1}}(a,b;z)

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(a\oplus b)_{1,q^{-1}}^{n}\frac{z^{n}}{[n]_{q^{-1}}!}

\displaystyle=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}q^{-\binom{n}{2}}(b\oplus a)_{1,q}^{n}\frac{z^{n}}{q^{-\binom{n}{2}}[n]_{q}!}

\displaystyle=\mathrm{E}_{q}(b,a;z)

and this proves 2. Statements 3 and 4 arise easily by making
a=1
and
b=-q
and then applying 1.
∎

Example 5
.

The solution of the functional equation

f(3x)-f(2x)=xf(x)+xf(x/2),\ f(0)=1

(67)

is

f(x)=\mathop{\mbox{{E}}}\nolimits_{5,-6}(1,1,1/2;x)=\sum_{n=0}^{\infty}(1\oplus 1)_{1,1/2}^{n}\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{5,-6}!},

convergent in
\mathbb{R}
, since
(2/3,3)\in S_{1}
.

From Theorem
29
we obtain the solution of the
(s,t)
-analog of the Ambartsumian equation

y^{\prime}=-y(x)+\frac{1}{v}y\left(\frac{1}{v}x\right),\ v>1,

which describes the surface brightness in Astronomy
[
37
,
38
]
. A
q
-analog of this equation can be found in
[
40
]
. In the following theorem the
(s,t)
-analog of the Ambartsumian equation is shown.

Theorem 33
.

The solution of the
(s,t)
-Ambartsumian equation

\mathbf{D}_{s,t}y(x)=-y(x)+\frac{1}{v}y\left(\frac{1}{v}x\right),\ v>1,

is

\mathrm{E}_{s,t}(-1,v^{-1},v^{-1};x)=\xi+\xi\sum_{n=1}^{\infty}\prod_{k=1}^{n}(v^{-k}-1)\frac{x^{n}}{\mathopen{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\{\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}{n}\mathclose{{\mathchoice{\hbox{$\displaystyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\textstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}{\hbox{$\scriptscriptstyle\left\}\vbox to0.0pt{}\right.$}}}}_{s,t}!}.
